As a consequence of the inf–sup condition established in Proposition 2, we derive the following auxiliary result.

Lemma 5

Assume the regularity on the data as in Assumption 1. Let (U, V) be the unique solution to (24), whose regularity is stated in Proposition 1, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(U_{\boldsymbol{h}},V_{\boldsymbol{h}})$$\end{document}(Uh,Vh) be the unique discrete solution to (25). Suppose also that35\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \cdot (c^2 \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} U) \in H^1(0,T;L^2(\varOmega )). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∇x·(c2∇xU)∈H1(0,T;L2(Ω)).Then, it holds\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}U- U_{\boldsymbol{h}}, \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}V - V_{\boldsymbol{h}})\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)} \\ &\quad \quad \le \beta \bigl (\Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \partial _t V \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \\ &\quad \quad \quad + \Vert (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \cdot (c^2 \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} U) \Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)} + \Vert c \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V \Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)}\bigr ), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇ΠhttU-Uh,Πx∇ΠhttV-Vh)‖Ve,h(QT)≤β(‖(Πx∇-Id)∂tV‖Le2(QT)+‖(Πhtt-Id)∇x·(c2∇xU)‖Le2(QT)+‖c∇xΠx∇(Πhtt-Id)V‖Le2(QT)),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta := 2\sqrt{C_\varOmega ^2/c_0^2 + 4T^2}$$\end{document}β:=2CΩ2/c02+4T2 is the reciprocal of the inf–sup constant in Proposition 4.

Proof

Error Estimates in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)$$\end{document}Ve,h(QT) Norm

In the following, the notation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_1 \lesssim Q_2$$\end{document}Q1≲Q2 (resp. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_1 \gtrsim Q_2$$\end{document}Q1≳Q2) means that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_1$$\end{document}Q1 is bounded above (resp. below) by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa \,Q_2$$\end{document}κQ2, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa >0$$\end{document}κ>0 is a constant independent of the mesh sizes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_t$$\end{document}ht and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_{\boldsymbol{x}}$$\end{document}hx, as well as of the involved functions. We remark that, unlike in the previous sections, where the dependence on the final time T was explicitly stated, the hidden constants in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lesssim $$\end{document}≲ notation may also depend on T.

We adopt the notation, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_t, s_{\boldsymbol{x}} \ge 1$$\end{document}st,sx≥1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} H_{0,\bullet }^{s_t}(0,T)&:= H_{0,\bullet }^1(0,T) \cap H^{s_t}(0,T), \\ H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varOmega )&:= H_0^1(\varOmega ) \cap H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varOmega ). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}H0,∙t(0,T):=H0,∙1(0,T)∩Hst(0,T),H0x(Ω):=H01(Ω)∩Hsx(Ω).We proceed by deriving explicit error estimates under the following assumptions on the discrete spaces.

Assumption 3

(space discretization) The finite dimensional space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varOmega ) \subset H_0^1(\varOmega )$$\end{document}Shx(Ω)⊂H01(Ω) satisfies the following approximation property for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} 1$$\end{document}px≥1: there exists an operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}^{p_{\boldsymbol{x}}}: H_0^1(\varOmega )\rightarrow S_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varOmega )$$\end{document}Qhxx:H01(Ω)→Shx(Ω) such that, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W\in H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega )$$\end{document}W∈H0sx+1(Ω) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} (Q_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}^{p_{\boldsymbol{x}}} W - W) \Vert _{L^2(\varOmega )}&\lesssim h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}} \Vert W \Vert _{H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega )}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∇x(QhxxW-W)‖L2(Ω)≲hxx‖W‖Hsx+1(Ω).

Assumption 4

(time discretization) The finite dimensional space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} Remark 10

In the case of discrete spaces consisting of continuous piecewise polynomials on shape-regular, simplicial meshes of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varOmega $$\end{document}Ω and arbitrary meshes of (0, T), Assumptions 3 and 4 are both satisfied, e.g., with the quasi-interpolation operator defined in Section [10, §22.3]; see [10, Theorem 22.6]. For one-dimensional B-splines, Assumption 4 is satisfied, e.g., with the quasi-interpolant in [26, Theorem 18] (see also references therein). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\blacksquare $$\end{document}▪

Under Assumption 4, we have the following approximation result for the operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}$$\end{document}Πhtt.

Lemma 6

For all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W \in H_{0,\bullet }^{s_t+1}(0,T;L^2(\varOmega ))$$\end{document}W∈H0,∙st+1(0,T;L2(Ω)), with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le s_t \le p_t$$\end{document}1≤st≤pt and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_t$$\end{document}pt as in Assumption 4, it holds true that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert ( \varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) W \Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)} \lesssim h^{s_t+1}_t \Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} W \Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πhtt-Id)W‖Le2(QT)≲htst+1‖∂tst+1W‖Le2(QT).

Proof

We note that41\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert ( \varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})W \Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)}^2 = \int _\varOmega \Vert (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) W(\boldsymbol{x},\cdot )\Vert ^2_{L^2_e(0,T)} \textrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πhtt-Id)W‖Le2(QT)2=∫Ω‖(Πhtt-Id)W(x,·)‖Le2(0,T)2dx.We can apply the Aubin–Nitsche trick to derive the following estimate:42\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) w\Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)} \lesssim h_t \Vert \partial _t(\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})w \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)} \,\,\, \text {for all~} w\in H_{0,\bullet }^1(0,T), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πhtt-Id)w‖Le2(0,T)≲ht‖∂t(Πhtt-Id)w‖Le2(0,T)for allw∈H0,∙1(0,T),where we used the notation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}$$\end{document}Πhtt to also denote the elliptic projector acting on functions in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^1(0,T)$$\end{document}H1(0,T). Once we have proved (42), the result follows by inserting it within (41), and using Assumption 4.

To prove (42), we consider the dual problem: given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in L^2(0,T)$$\end{document}f∈L2(0,T), find \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in H^1_{0,\bullet }(0,T)$$\end{document}z∈H0,∙1(0,T) such that43\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\partial _t v, \partial _t z)_{L^2_e(0,T)} = (f, v)_{L^2_e(0,T)} \quad \text {for all~} v \in H^1_{0,\bullet }(0,T). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(∂tv,∂tz)Le2(0,T)=(f,v)Le2(0,T)for allv∈H0,∙1(0,T).By the Lax–Milgram lemma, this problem admits a unique solution. Moreover, using the Poincaré inequality and (9), we obtain44\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _t z \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)}^2 = (f,z)_{L^2_e(0,T)}&\le \Vert z \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)} \Vert f \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)} \\ &\lesssim \Vert \partial _t z\Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)} \Vert f \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)}. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂tz‖Le2(0,T)2=(f,z)Le2(0,T)≤‖z‖Le2(0,T)‖f‖Le2(0,T)≲‖∂tz‖Le2(0,T)‖f‖Le2(0,T).Moreover, the unique solution of (43) can be explicitly computed as the solution of\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \partial _t^2 z(t) + T^{-1} \partial _t z(t) = f(t) & t \in (0,T), \\ z(0) = 0, \quad \partial _t z(T) = 0, \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂t2z(t)+T-1∂tz(t)=f(t)t∈(0,T),z(0)=0,∂tz(T)=0,from which, also using (44), it follows that45\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _t^2 z \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)} \lesssim \Vert \partial _t z \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)} + \Vert f \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)} \lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂t2z‖Le2(0,T)≲‖∂tz‖Le2(0,T)+‖f‖Le2(0,T)≲‖f‖Le2(0,T).Now, let us take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})w$$\end{document}(Πhtt-Id)w is an admissible right-hand side test function since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w\in H^1_{0,\bullet }(0,T)$$\end{document}w∈H0,∙1(0,T). Then, using the definition of the elliptic projection \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}$$\end{document}Πhtt, we obtain that, for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z_{h_t} \in S_{h_t}(0,T)$$\end{document}zht∈Sht(0,T),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})w \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)}^2&= (\partial _t (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})w, \partial _t (z-z_{h_t}))_{L^2_e(0,T)}\\ &\le \Vert \partial _t (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})w \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)} \Vert \partial _t (z-z_{h_t}) \Vert _{L^2_e(0,T)}, \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πhtt-Id)w‖Le2(0,T)2=(∂t(Πhtt-Id)w,∂t(z-zht))Le2(0,T)≤‖∂t(Πhtt-Id)w‖Le2(0,T)‖∂t(z-zht)‖Le2(0,T),where in the last step we used the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. The following lemma establishes the approximation properties of compositions of the projectors needed to prove explicit convergence estimates.

Lemma 7

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le s_{\boldsymbol{x}} \le p_{\boldsymbol{x}}$$\end{document}1≤sx≤px and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le s_t \le p_t$$\end{document}1≤st≤pt, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_{\boldsymbol{x}}$$\end{document}px and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_t$$\end{document}pt as in Assumptions 3 and 4, respectively. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇-Id)∂tV‖Le2(QT)≲hxx‖∂tV‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω));(ii)for any U such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \cdot (c^2 \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} U) \in H_{0,\bullet }^{s_t+1}(0,T;L^2(\varOmega ))$$\end{document}∇x·(c2∇xU)∈H0,∙st+1(0,T;L2(Ω)), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \cdot (c^2 \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} U) \Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)} \lesssim h_t^{s_t+1}\Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \cdot (c^2 \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} U) \Vert _{L^2(Q_T)}; for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&U \in H_{0,\bullet }^{s_t+1}(0,T;H^1(\varOmega )) \cap H^1(0,T;H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega )), \\ &V \in H_{0,\bullet }^{s_t+1}(0,T;H_0^1(\varOmega )) \cap H^1(0,T;H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega )), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}U∈H0,∙st+1(0,T;H1(Ω))∩H1(0,T;H0sx+1(Ω)),V∈H0,∙st+1(0,T;H01(Ω))∩H1(0,T;H0sx+1(Ω)), it holds true that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\Vert ((\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) U,(\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V) \Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,{\boldsymbol{h}}}(Q_T)} \\&\quad \quad \quad \lesssim h_t^{s_t}\Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} U\Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^1(\varOmega ))}+ h^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\boldsymbol{x}} \Vert \partial _t U \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}} + 1}(\varOmega ))} \\ &\quad \quad \quad \quad + h_t^{s_t}\Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} V\Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^1(\varOmega ))} + h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\Vert \partial _t V \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))}. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖((Πx∇Πhtt-Id)U,(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)V)‖Ve,h(QT)≲htt‖∂tst+1U‖L2(0,T;H1(Ω))+hxx‖∂tU‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω))+htt‖∂tst+1V‖L2(0,T;H1(Ω))+hxx‖∂tV‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω)).

Proof

For the estimate in (i), we apply the Poincaré inequality in space\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_x} - {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})\partial _tV \Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)} \lesssim \Vert c \nabla _x(\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_x} - {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})\partial _tV\Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇-Id)∂tV‖Le2(QT)≲‖c∇x(Πx∇-Id)∂tV‖Le2(QT),the approximation property in (31), and Assumption 3:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\Vert c \nabla _x(\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_x} - {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})\partial _tV\Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)} \\ &\quad \lesssim \inf _{W_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}} \in S_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varOmega ) \otimes L^2(0,T)} \Vert c\nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} (W_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}} - \partial _t V) \Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)} \\ &\quad \lesssim h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}} \left( \int _0^T \Vert \partial _t V(\cdot ,s) \Vert ^2_{H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega )} e^{-s/T} \, \textrm{d}s\right) ^{\frac{1}{2}} \\ &\quad \lesssim h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}} \Vert \partial _t V \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))}. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖c∇x(Πx∇-Id)∂tV‖Le2(QT)≲infWhx∈Shx(Ω)⊗L2(0,T)‖c∇x(Whx-∂tV)‖Le2(QT)≲hxx∫0T‖∂tV(·,s)‖Hsx+1(Ω)2e-s/Tds12≲hxx‖∂tV‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω)).To obtain (ii), we simply use Lemma 6, while (iii) is obtained by combining the stability property in (33) with Lemma 6.

In the subsequent steps of the proof, we repeatedly use the following identities, which follow from Lemma 4:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}&= \varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}\\ &= \varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}+ \varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}(\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}} - {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \\ &= \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) + \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}} - {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Πx∇Πhtt-Id=ΠhttΠx∇-Id=Πhtt-Id+Πhtt(Πx∇-Id)=Πx∇(Πhtt-Id)+Πx∇-Id.To prove the estimate in (iv), we compute\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert \partial _t(\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})U\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \\ &\quad \le \Vert \partial _t (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} + \Vert \partial _t \varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}(\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \\&\quad \le \Vert \partial _t (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} + \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \partial _t U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}}, \\ &\quad \lesssim \Vert \partial _t (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} + \Vert c \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \partial _t U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂t(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)U‖Le2(QT)≤‖∂t(Πhtt-Id)U‖Le2(QT)+‖∂tΠhtt(Πx∇-Id)U‖Le2(QT)≤‖∂t(Πhtt-Id)U‖Le2(QT)+‖(Πx∇-Id)∂tU‖Le2(QT),≲‖∂t(Πhtt-Id)U‖Le2(QT)+‖c∇x(Πx∇-Id)∂tU‖Le2(QT),where we also used the stability property in (34), the Poincaré inequality in space and the commutativity properties in Lemma 4. Then, (iv) follows from the approximation properties (31) and (32), combined with Assumptions 3 and 4.

For the inequality in (v), the Poincaré inequality in space, the stability property in (33), and the commutativity properties in Lemma 4 imply\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \\ &\quad \le \Vert \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} + \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \\ &\quad \lesssim \Vert \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} + \Vert \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \\ &\quad \lesssim \Vert (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} V\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} + \Vert \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)V‖Le2(QT)≤‖Πx∇(Πhtt-Id)V‖Le2(QT)+‖(Πx∇-Id)V‖Le2(QT)≲‖∇xΠx∇(Πhtt-Id)V‖Le2(QT)+‖∇x(Πx∇-Id)V‖Le2(QT)≲‖(Πhtt-Id)∇xV‖Le2(QT)+‖∇x(Πx∇-Id)V‖Le2(QT).We conclude with Lemma 6 and the approximation property (31) of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}$$\end{document}Πx∇, combined with Assumption 3.

For (vi), we proceed similarly, and compute, with the stability property in (33) and the commutativity properties in Lemma 4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert c \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})U\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \\ &\quad \le \Vert c \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}(\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} + \Vert c \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \\ &\quad \le \Vert (\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) c\nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}}+ \Vert c \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})U \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖c∇x(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)U‖Le2(QT)≤‖c∇xΠx∇(Πhtt-Id)U‖Le2(QT)+‖c∇x(Πx∇-Id)U‖Le2(QT)≤‖(Πhtt-Id)c∇xU‖Le2(QT)+‖c∇x(Πx∇-Id)U‖Le2(QT),and conclude exactly as for (v).

For (vii), we use Lemma 3 to first obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _t (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V\Vert _{\mathcal {N}^\varOmega _{e,{\boldsymbol{h}}}}&\lesssim \Vert \partial _t (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂t(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)V‖Ne,hΩ≲‖∂t(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)V‖Le2(QT),and then proceed as in (iv).

Finally, the projection error estimate in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)$$\end{document}Ve,h(QT) norm stated in (viii) readily follows from (iv)–(vii) and the Poincaré inequality applied in space and in time. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 11

Under a standard elliptic regularity assumption in space, improved estimates for the projection error associated with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}$$\end{document}Πx∇ in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2_e(Q_T)$$\end{document}Le2(QT) norm can be derived using a duality argument. We emphasize that this assumption is not needed for the error estimate in Theorem 5. However, in Section 4.2.3 below, we rely on it, along with the resulting improved projection error estimates, to derive error bounds in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2 norm. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\blacksquare $$\end{document}▪

Remark 12

When U and V are sufficiently smooth, we deduce from Lemma 5 and (i)–(iii) in Lemma 7 that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}U- U_{\boldsymbol{h}}, \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}V - V_{\boldsymbol{h}})\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)} \lesssim C(U,V) \left( h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}}+h_t^{s_t+1}\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇ΠhttU-Uh,Πx∇ΠhttV-Vh)‖Ve,h(QT)≲C(U,V)hxx+htst+1,where we have absorbed the dependence on the analytical solution (U, V) and its derivatives into the positive constant C(U, V), while, for the projection error, the best achievable estimate in the norm \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \cdot \Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖·‖Ve,h(QT) is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert ((\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) U,(\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) V)\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)}\lesssim C(U,V) \left( h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}}+h_t^{s_t}\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖((Πx∇Πhtt-Id)U,(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)V)‖Ve,h(QT)≲C(U,V)hxx+htt.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\blacksquare $$\end{document}▪

Combining Lemmas 5 and 7 yields the main result.

Theorem 5

Let us assume the regularity on the data as in Assumption 1. Proof

For all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(W_{\boldsymbol{h}},Z_{\boldsymbol{h}}) \in (Q_{\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T))^2$$\end{document}(Wh,Zh)∈(Qh(QT))2, with the triangle inequality, we deduce47\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \Vert (U,V) - (U_{\boldsymbol{h}},V_{\boldsymbol{h}})\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)}&\le \Vert (U,V) - (W_{\boldsymbol{h}},Z_{\boldsymbol{h}})\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)} \\ &\quad + \Vert (W_{\boldsymbol{h}},Z_{\boldsymbol{h}}) - (U_{\boldsymbol{h}},V_{\boldsymbol{h}})\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)}. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(U,V)-(Uh,Vh)‖Ve,h(QT)≤‖(U,V)-(Wh,Zh)‖Ve,h(QT)+‖(Wh,Zh)-(Uh,Vh)‖Ve,h(QT).We choose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_{\boldsymbol{h}}:= \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}U$$\end{document}Wh:=Πx∇ΠhttU and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z_{\boldsymbol{h}}:= \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}V$$\end{document}Zh:=Πx∇ΠhttV where the projectors are defined in (29) and (30). Then, we obtain the estimate in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \cdot \Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖·‖Ve,h(QT) norm by using (viii) in Lemma 7 to estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert (U,V) - (W_{\boldsymbol{h}},Z_{\boldsymbol{h}})\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖(U,V)-(Wh,Zh)‖Ve,h(QT), and combining Lemma 5 with (i)–(iii) in Lemma 7 to estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert (W_{\boldsymbol{h}},Z_{\boldsymbol{h}}) - (U_{\boldsymbol{h}},V_{\boldsymbol{h}})\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖(Wh,Zh)-(Uh,Vh)‖Ve,h(QT).

For the second estimate, the triangle inequality, together with the definition (28) of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} Remark 13

As pointed out in Remark 12, the projection error term dominates in (47), yielding\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (U,V) - (U_{\boldsymbol{h}},V_{\boldsymbol{h}})\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)} \lesssim C(U,V)\left( h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}}+h_t^{s_t}\right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(U,V)-(Uh,Vh)‖Ve,h(QT)≲C(U,V)hxx+htt,where, as in Remark 12, we have absorbed the dependence on the analytical solution (U, V) and its derivatives into the positive constant C(U, V). Error Estimates in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2 Norm

In this section, we derive improved error estimates for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert U - U_{\boldsymbol{h}}\Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖U-Uh‖Le2(QT) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert V- V_{\boldsymbol{h}}\Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖V-Vh‖Le2(QT) under the following standard elliptic regularity assumption in space.

Assumption 6

(elliptic regularity) We assume that, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g \in L^2(\varOmega )$$\end{document}g∈L2(Ω), the unique solution to the problem: find \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi \in H_0^1(\varOmega )$$\end{document}φ∈H01(Ω) such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (c^2 \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \varphi , \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \psi )_{L^2(\varOmega )} = (g,\psi )_{L^2(\varOmega ) \quad } \quad \text {for all~} \psi \in H^1_0(\varOmega ) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(c2∇xφ,∇xψ)L2(Ω)=(g,ψ)L2(Ω)for allψ∈H01(Ω)belongs to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^2(\varOmega )$$\end{document}H2(Ω) and satisfies the stability estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \varphi \Vert _{H^2(\varOmega )} \lesssim \Vert g \Vert _{L^2(\varOmega )}$$\end{document}‖φ‖H2(Ω)≲‖g‖L2(Ω).

Remark 15

Assumption 6 is satisfied when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c^2$$\end{document}c2 is a Lipschitz function in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varOmega $$\end{document}Ω, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varOmega $$\end{document}Ω is convex and Lipschitz, or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial \varOmega $$\end{document}∂Ω is of class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^{1,1}$$\end{document}C1,1. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\blacksquare $$\end{document}▪

Under Assumptions 3 and 6, a standard duality argument yields49\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})W\Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)}\lesssim h_{\boldsymbol{x}}\Vert \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}}(\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}})W\Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇-Id)W‖Le2(QT)≲hx‖∇x(Πx∇-Id)W‖Le2(QT)for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W\in L^2(0,T;H^1_0(\varOmega ))$$\end{document}W∈L2(0,T;H01(Ω)), which leads to error estimates in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2_e(Q_T)$$\end{document}Le2(QT) norm with improved converge rates in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_{\boldsymbol{x}}$$\end{document}hx. To achieve this, we first refine the projection error estimates (i) and (v) of Lemma 7.

Lemma 8

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le s_{\boldsymbol{x}} \le p_{\boldsymbol{x}}$$\end{document}1≤sx≤px and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le s_t \le p_t$$\end{document}1≤st≤pt , with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_{\boldsymbol{x}}$$\end{document}px and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_{t}$$\end{document}pt as in Assumptions 3 and 4, respectively. Then, if also Assumption 6 is satisfied, we obtain (i)for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W \in H^1(0,T;H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}} +1}(\varOmega ))$$\end{document}W∈H1(0,T;H0sx+1(Ω)), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \partial _t W \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \lesssim h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}\Vert \partial _t W \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))}; \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇-Id)∂tW‖Le2(QT)≲hxsx+1‖∂tW‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω));(ii)for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W \in H_{0,\bullet }^{s_t+1}(0,T;H_0^1(\varOmega )) \cap L^2(0,T;H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))$$\end{document}W∈H0,∙st+1(0,T;H01(Ω))∩L2(0,T;H0sx+1(Ω)), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) W\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}}&\lesssim h_t^{s_t+1}\Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} W \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^1(\varOmega ))} \\ &\quad + h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}\Vert W \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}} + 1}(\varOmega ))}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)W‖Le2(QT)≲htst+1‖∂tst+1W‖L2(0,T;H1(Ω))+hxsx+1‖W‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω)).

(i)for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W \in H^1(0,T;H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}} +1}(\varOmega ))$$\end{document}W∈H1(0,T;H0sx+1(Ω)), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \partial _t W \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \lesssim h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}\Vert \partial _t W \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))}; \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇-Id)∂tW‖Le2(QT)≲hxsx+1‖∂tW‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω));

for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W \in H^1(0,T;H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}} +1}(\varOmega ))$$\end{document}W∈H1(0,T;H0sx+1(Ω)), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}-{{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \partial _t W \Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}} \lesssim h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}\Vert \partial _t W \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))}; \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇-Id)∂tW‖Le2(QT)≲hxsx+1‖∂tW‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω));

(ii)for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W \in H_{0,\bullet }^{s_t+1}(0,T;H_0^1(\varOmega )) \cap L^2(0,T;H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))$$\end{document}W∈H0,∙st+1(0,T;H01(Ω))∩L2(0,T;H0sx+1(Ω)), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) W\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}}&\lesssim h_t^{s_t+1}\Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} W \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^1(\varOmega ))} \\ &\quad + h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}\Vert W \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}} + 1}(\varOmega ))}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)W‖Le2(QT)≲htst+1‖∂tst+1W‖L2(0,T;H1(Ω))+hxsx+1‖W‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω)).

for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W \in H_{0,\bullet }^{s_t+1}(0,T;H_0^1(\varOmega )) \cap L^2(0,T;H_0^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))$$\end{document}W∈H0,∙st+1(0,T;H01(Ω))∩L2(0,T;H0sx+1(Ω)), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) W\Vert _{{L^2_e(Q_T)}}&\lesssim h_t^{s_t+1}\Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} W \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^1(\varOmega ))} \\ &\quad + h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}\Vert W \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}} + 1}(\varOmega ))}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)W‖Le2(QT)≲htst+1‖∂tst+1W‖L2(0,T;H1(Ω))+hxsx+1‖W‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω)).

Proof

The proofs of (i) and (ii) proceed analogously to those of (i) and (v) in Lemma 7, respectively, except that, instead of the Poincaré inequality in space, we use (49) to estimate terms of the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert (\varPi _{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}^\nabla - {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) \cdot \Vert _{L^2_e(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖(Πhx∇-Id)·‖Le2(QT), which gives the extra power of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_{\boldsymbol{x}}$$\end{document}hx. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We now prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 7

In addition to the assumptions of Theorem 5, suppose that Assumption 6 is satisfied. Then, we have50\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\Vert U - U_{\boldsymbol{h}}\Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)} \\ &\quad \lesssim h^{s_t+1}_t \Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} U \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^1(\varOmega ))} + h^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}_{\boldsymbol{x}} \Vert U \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))} \\ &\quad \quad + h_t^{s_t+1} (\Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \cdot (c^2 \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} U) \Vert _{L^2(Q_T)} + \Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} V\Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^1(\varOmega ))}), \\ &\quad \quad + h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1} \Vert \partial _t V \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))}, \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖U-Uh‖Le2(QT)≲htst+1‖∂tst+1U‖L2(0,T;H1(Ω))+hxsx+1‖U‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω))+htst+1(‖∂tst+1∇x·(c2∇xU)‖L2(QT)+‖∂tst+1V‖L2(0,T;H1(Ω))),+hxsx+1‖∂tV‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω)),51\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\Vert V - V_{\boldsymbol{h}}\Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)} \\ &\quad \lesssim h^{s_t+1}_t \Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} V \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^1(\varOmega ))} + h^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}_{\boldsymbol{x}} \Vert \partial _t V \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))} \\ &\quad \quad + h_t^{s_t+1} (\Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} \cdot (c^2 \nabla _{\boldsymbol{x}} U) \Vert _{L^2(Q_T)} + \Vert \partial _t^{s_t+1} V\Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^1(\varOmega ))}). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖V-Vh‖Le2(QT)≲htst+1‖∂tst+1V‖L2(0,T;H1(Ω))+hxsx+1‖∂tV‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω))+htst+1(‖∂tst+1∇x·(c2∇xU)‖L2(QT)+‖∂tst+1V‖L2(0,T;H1(Ω))).

Proof

Using the triangle inequality, the Poincaré inequality in time, and the definition (28) of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \cdot \Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖·‖Ve,h(QT), we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert U - U_{\boldsymbol{h}}\Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)}&\le \Vert \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}U - U_{\boldsymbol{h}} \Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)} + \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) U\Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)} \\ &\hspace{-2cm} \le \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}U- U_{\boldsymbol{h}}, \varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}V - V_{\boldsymbol{h}})\Vert _{\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)} + \Vert (\varPi ^{\nabla }_{h_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\varPi _{h_t}^{\partial _t}- {{\,\textrm{Id}\,}}) U\Vert _{L_e^2(Q_T)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖U-Uh‖Le2(QT)≤‖Πx∇ΠhttU-Uh‖Le2(QT)+‖(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)U‖Le2(QT)≤‖(Πx∇ΠhttU-Uh,Πx∇ΠhttV-Vh)‖Ve,h(QT)+‖(Πx∇Πhtt-Id)U‖Le2(QT).We derive (50) combining Lemma 5, (i), (ii) of Lemma 8, and (ii), (iii) of Lemma 7. Inequality (51) is obtained similarly, except that, in the first step, the Poincaré inequality is not needed. We note that, in the derivation of (51), the term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}\Vert V \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}} + 1}(\varOmega ))}$$\end{document}hxsx+1‖V‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω)) arising in (ii) of Lemma 8 is estimated by a Poincaré inequality in time and then absorbed into the term \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}_{\boldsymbol{x}} \Vert \partial _t V \Vert _{L^2(0,T;H^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}(\varOmega ))}$$\end{document}hxsx+1‖∂tV‖L2(0,T;Hsx+1(Ω)). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 16

Similarly to Remark 13, we observe that, due to the norm equivalence (10) in Remark 2, we readily have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert U - U_{\boldsymbol{h}}\Vert _{L^2(Q_T)}+ \Vert V - V_{\boldsymbol{h}}\Vert _{L^2(Q_T)}\lesssim C(U,V) \left( h_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{s_{\boldsymbol{x}}+1}+h_t^{s_t+1}\right) . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖U-Uh‖L2(QT)+‖V-Vh‖L2(QT)≲C(U,V)hxsx+1+htst+1.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\blacksquare $$\end{document}▪

Numerical Results

In this section, we present two numerical tests in one space dimension to validate the stability and convergence of the proposed method.1 The first test involves a smooth solution. We demonstrate unconditional stability and observe convergence rates using standard space–time tensor product discretizations. In the second test, we consider a singular solution and still observe convergence, even though this case is not covered by our theory.

An important aspect not addressed in this work is the efficient implementation of the space–time method, including its potential reformulation as a time-marching scheme; we refer to [14, §5.1] for a detailed discussion.

Smooth Solution

As a first numerical test, we consider problem (1) in the domain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} Convergence To validate the convergence results established in Theorems 5 and 7, we compute the relative errors in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)$$\end{document}Ve,h(QT) norm for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(U_{\boldsymbol{h}}, V_{\boldsymbol{h}})$$\end{document}(Uh,Vh), and in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(Q_T)$$\end{document}L2(QT) norm for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_{\boldsymbol{h}}$$\end{document}Uh. When using splines in both space and time with equal polynomial degree p and uniform mesh size \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h = h_t = h_x$$\end{document}h=ht=hx, Theorem 5 predicts a convergence rate of order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {O}(h^p)$$\end{document}O(hp) in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {V}_{e,\boldsymbol{h}}(Q_T)$$\end{document}Ve,h(QT) norm. Furthermore, from Theorem 7, we expect a convergence rate of order \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {O}(h^{p+1})$$\end{document}O(hp+1) in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2 norm. These theoretical rates are confirmed numerically in Figures 2 and 3 for both maximal regularity and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^1$$\end{document}C1-splines. As expected, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert V-V_{\boldsymbol{h}}\Vert _{L^2(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖V-Vh‖L2(QT), we obtained the same convergence rates as for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert U-U_{\boldsymbol{h}}\Vert _{L^2(Q_T)}$$\end{document}‖U-Uh‖L2(QT). Singular Solution

As a second test, we consider the initial value boundary problem (1) with the non-smooth traveling wave solution of [7, §7, Problem 3]. The domain is given by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q_T = \varOmega \times (0,1)$$\end{document}QT=Ω×(0,1), with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varOmega = (-1.5,1.5)$$\end{document}Ω=(-1.5,1.5), and we set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F(x,t)=0$$\end{document}F(x,t)=0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c(x)=1$$\end{document}c(x)=1. The initial conditions are chosen so that the exact solution is53\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} U(x,t) = \omega (x-t+1) 1\!\!1_{>0}(x-t+1), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}U(x,t)=ω(x-t+1)1>0(x-t+1),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\!\!1_{>0}$$\end{document}1>0 is the indicator function of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(0,\infty )$$\end{document}(0,∞), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega (s):= e^{-20(s-0.1)^2} - e^{-20(s+0.1)^2}$$\end{document}ω(s):=e-20(s-0.1)2-e-20(s+0.1)2.

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t \in (0,1)$$\end{document}t∈(0,1), the solution satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|U(\pm 1.5,t)| \le 10^{-17}$$\end{document}|U(±1.5,t)|≤10-17, so we impose homogeneous boundary conditions in practice. The solution U(x, t) is non-smooth along the line \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x-~t+1=0$$\end{document}x-t+1=0, due to a jump discontinuity in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _t U$$\end{document}∂tU across that line (since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega '(0)~=~8e^{-0.2} \ne 0$$\end{document}ω′(0)=8e-0.2≠0). Consequently, U lies in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^{3/2-\varepsilon }(Q_T)$$\end{document}H3/2-ε(QT) for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0, but not in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^{3/2}(Q_T)$$\end{document}H3/2(QT). Therefore, the velocity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V=\partial _t U$$\end{document}V=∂tU fails to belong to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^1(Q_T)$$\end{document}H1(QT), so this example lies outside the theoretical framework of Theorem 5. We test the convergence of the discrete scheme (25) using uniform meshes and maximal regularity splines of degree p in both space and time. Although the regularity assumptions on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V=\partial _t U$$\end{document}V=∂tU required by Theorem 5 are not satisfied, we observe numerical convergence rates in practice that match the expected ones: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {O}(h^{3/2})$$\end{document}O(h3/2) for U and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {O}(h^{1/2})$$\end{document}O(h1/2) for V. Mixed Boundary Conditions

In this example, we consider a problem with mixed Dirichlet and impedance boundary conditions in space. Although this configuration is not covered by our theoretical analysis, we show how our numerical scheme can be adapted to handle impedance boundary conditions and present a numerical experiment that shows optimal convergence rates also in this setting.

The test problem we consider reads as follows: find U such that54\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \partial _t^2 U(x,t) - \partial _x^2 U(x,t) = \frac{\pi ^2}{2} e^{- \frac{\pi }{2} t} \sin \left( \frac{\pi }{2} x\right) & (x,t) \in (0,\frac{1}{2}) \times (0,1), \\ U(0,t) = 0 & t \in (0,1], \\ \partial _x U(\frac{1}{2},t) + \partial _t U(\frac{1}{2},t) = 0 & t \in (0,1], \\ U(x,0) = \sin \left( \frac{\pi }{2}x\right) & x \in (0,\frac{1}{2}), \\ \partial _t U(x,0) = -\frac{\pi }{2} \sin \left( \frac{\pi }{2}x\right) & x \in (0,\frac{1}{2}). \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂t2U(x,t)-∂x2U(x,t)=π22e-π2tsinπ2x(x,t)∈(0,12)×(0,1),U(0,t)=0t∈(0,1],∂xU(12,t)+∂tU(12,t)=0t∈(0,1],U(x,0)=sinπ2xx∈(0,12),∂tU(x,0)=-π2sinπ2xx∈(0,12).The analytical solution is given by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U(x,t) = e^{-\frac{\pi }{2} t} \sin \left( \frac{\pi }{2} x\right) $$\end{document}U(x,t)=e-π2tsinπ2x.

As highlighted in Remark 8, although the exponential weights are necessary for the theoretical stability analysis, they do not appear to play a significant role from a numerical point of view. Conclusion

In this work, we introduced a conforming space–time tensor product discretization method for the wave equation based on a first-order-in-time variational formulation with exponential weights in time. The weights play a fundamental role in the stability analysis, allowing to prove unconditional well-posedness and optimal convergence rates with considerable flexibility in the choice of discrete spaces. However, from a practical point of view, they may be unnecessary. For instance, for the first-order-in-time formulation, they are proven not to be needed in the case of continuous piecewise polynomial approximations in time [18, 20], or when using maximal regularity splines in time [14]. Whether this is true in general remains an open question.
